Manually read both characters' full regenerated text: no leaked brackets or authoring notes, victim name ("Captain Odessa Vane") consistent with `game_overview`, no cross-character contamination, tone/register consistent with each character's existing voice (Beachcomber's accomplice branch keeps the scavenger/tide imagery established in their other fields; Netmaker's ties back to the debt-to-Vane motive already established elsewhere in that character). `validate_package_characters()`'s trigger (the same one from Addendum 32) picked up the field writes automatically and flipped `generation_status` back to `completed` with `needs_review_at` cleared — no manual status update needed, confirming the self-heal path Addendum 31 relies on works end-to-end on a genuinely different package.

**Nothing new shipped here** — this addendum is a clean confirmation that Addendum 31's detector and fix mechanism work as designed on an unrelated live purchase, not a new bug or gap. Recorded per this ADR's own logging convention (every sweep hit gets an addendum, clean sweeps don't) and because a CHANGELOG entry was warranted (real content fix, unlike the single-character content edit in Addendum 33 which stayed ADR-only).

## Addendum 36 (2026-09-09): wired `missing_role_branch_content` into the auto-heal pipeline — the "revisit if this recurs" bar from Addendum 31 was met

Jonathan asked, after Addendum 35's manual fix: "is there anything we need to put in to make sure this doesn't happen in the future or is it just the way it is?" Addendum 31 (2026-09-08) had already drawn this exact line: it built detection for this defect class but deliberately left the fix alert-only, reasoning "only 2 live instances found... wiring a new auto-heal path for a defect this rare is more standing machinery than the evidence currently supports. Revisit if this recurs." Addendum 35 is that recurrence — a second, unrelated package, one day later. Answered with a build, not a shrug: the generation-side root cause (why a character batch call occasionally drops one field) stays a known, accepted, unchased rarity per Addendum 31's original call; the self-heal gap was the part actually worth closing, and it was already flagged as a deferred decision, not a settled one.

**What shipped:** `auto-remediate-packages` (ADR-0047/0061) already self-heals three other character-scope defect classes (`identity_contamination`, `slip_culprit_leak`, `meta_text_leak`) by delegating to `regenerate-child-content`, each dispatched from its own `list_packages_with_*` RPC. `missing_role_branch_content` had no such RPC — only `package_completion_blocking_defects()`'s flat `text[]` of `"class.character"` strings, built for the completion-gate triggers, not for a delegation loop that needs a precise per-character field list. Added `list_packages_with_missing_role_branch_content()` (migration `20260909180000_add_missing_role_branch_content_list_detector.sql`), the missing counterpart: one row per (package, character) naming exactly which of the 6 accomplice/reveal-confession fields are missing on that peer, using the identical peer-existence gate as `package_completion_blocking_defects()`. Verified byte-for-byte equivalence before relying on it: a query diffing every `package_id`+`character_name` hit between the new function and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` across the last 45 days returned zero rows in both directions.

Wired it as the 4th delegated class in `auto-remediate-packages`: new `DefectClass` entry, `DETECTOR_RPC` mapping, and `handleMissingRoleBranchContent()` — simpler than the other three's handlers because the detector row already carries the exact missing-field list for that one character (no `DELEGATE_DEFAULT_FIELDS` seed, no auto-widening needed). `regenerate-child-content`'s `DefectHint` type gained the new literal purely for correct `auto_remediation_log` labeling — `autoExpand`/`effectiveFieldsFor` both already no-op for any hint they don't explicitly branch on, which is exactly the right behavior here (the caller already computed the precise fields; no widening should happen).

Added the new class to the 5-minute held-only sweep's `classes` filter (`cron.alter_job` on job id 9, `auto-remediate-held-packages`) alongside the other three delegated classes, so a future instance self-heals within ~5 minutes of hitting `needs_review` instead of waiting for the 30-minute full sweep or a human running `sweep` manually. The 30-day `WINDOW_DAYS` bound (unchanged, applies to every class) keeps the ~13 pre-2026-08-11 historical instances Addendum 31 explicitly decided not to backfill out of scope by construction, not a special-cased exclusion.

**Verified live, not just read the code:** deployed both edge functions (`regenerate-child-content` v16, `auto-remediate-packages` v11 — confirmed `verify_jwt: true` preserved on both, per [[feedback_git_push_not_deployed]]'s standing lesson that a deploy must be checked, not assumed). Ran `auto-remediate-packages` in `dry_run` mode three ways — filtered to just the new class, unfiltered across all classes, and with the exact `only_needs_review` + `classes` payload the cron now sends — all three completed cleanly with `considered: 0` (correct: Addendum 35's fix already cleared the only live instance, and the new list function independently confirms zero packages currently need this repair).

**Not done here, deliberately:** no change to the generation-side root cause (still an accepted rarity per Addendum 31); no backfill of the historical pre-2026-08-11 packages (same reasoning as Addendum 31 — already played); no LLM-based "why did this field drop" detector (never proposed, not the gap that was open).

## Addendum 37 (2026-09-10): a truncated LLM reply propagated a broken character name into a live paid package — fixed; roster-count mismatch alert traced to the same truncation, left open as a scope question for Jonathan

**Trigger:** sweep on 3 same-day purchases (Terminus 13, `787bfbba-547a-4a44-a555-0b98b4ba525b`/`e86e0bee-560e-435b-910a-5e636e0421bc`; The Last Caravan Of Riften, `84e09db1-9cd6-4a15-96cd-d1a2ce174363`/`a286b582-b5fc-4d57-a6ef-76e7b006e3f5`; Murder At The Rhinestone Rodeo, `778bb0d8-e340-441b-a9f1-d1427c4545ba`/`8198170c-8e0d-4f41-88b9-06da7553ead0` — all EN, `mystery_style='detective'`, all post-ADR-0074 Sonnet 5), paired with a same-day GitHub health-check alert (🔴 roster-count mismatch, Terminus 13, `approved=14, actual=15`).

**Investigation.** All three named detectors (meta-text leak, victim mismatch, unresolved victim name) and the broader `list_packages_with_*` suite (evidence-culprit spoiler, final-statement confession leak, identity conflicts, missing-role-branch content, self-directed questions, slip-culprit leak, unconfessed culprit, victim-as-character, characters-absent-from-conversation) all came back clean on all three packages, as did `package_completion_blocking_defects()`. Manual victim-name cross-check (every character's `background` against the victim's name) passed on all three (15/15, 10/10, 8/8). Manual secret-vs-background read of murderer/accomplice/red-herring characters across all three found no inversions (the Addendum 33 bug class) and no leaked authoring notes.

The roster-count alert led somewhere real, though. Reproduced with the shipped `scripts/detect-roster-mismatches.mjs` (not a hand re-derivation): Terminus 13's `approved_concept_message_id` message — the customer's own concept-approval reply, sent 2026-09-09 22:31, 5 minutes before purchase — is a numbered 28-character roster that the model's own reply cuts off mid-word at character #15: `"15. **Scavenger Row/R"`, no continuation message follows it in the conversation (it's the last assistant turn). `extractRosterFromMessage` correctly parses only the 14 complete entries before the truncation, hence `approved=14`. The delivered package has 15 `mystery_characters` rows — the 14 complete ones plus a 15th generated **from the truncated line itself**: `character_name = 'Scavenger Row/R'`, a broken half-written dual-name with no surname, matching the exact truncation point. The generated `background`/`introduction`/`description` for that character are otherwise complete, well-written, and internally consistent (using "Row" throughout as the working name) — only the `character_name` field and one `**Name:**` header line embedded in `background` carried the truncated string forward. Checked for wider blast radius (other characters' `relationships`/`background`, `game_overview`, `detective_script`, `host_guide`, `evidence_cards`, `materials`, `quick_reference`) — zero other references to the broken name; fully contained to one row, two fields.

**Fixed** (plain SQL text update, no Anthropic call, no permission needed): `character_name` → `Scavenger Row/Robin Teague`, and the matching `**Name:**` line inside `background`. Re-verified: `character_name` and header both read the completed name; no other field references the old truncated string.

**Left open, not decided unilaterally — the 28-vs-15 scope question.** The customer explicitly asked for 28 players three separate times across the conversation (initial brief: "for 28 players with full scripts"; a later message: "we're building for 28 scripted characters"; the truncated approval message's own header: "Character List (28 players)"). `conversations.player_count` reads 15 and the package was generated to match 15, not 28 — consistent with the documented, still-open [[project_stale_snapshot_never_recaptures]] gap (`player_count` is a form-captured value that chat-only scope changes don't update), compounded here by the reply truncation cutting the concept short at almost exactly the stale count. Whether the customer actually wants a 15-character game (deliberately scaled down at checkout, unrecorded in this conversation) or paid for and is expecting the 28-character world they spent two sessions designing is not something the data resolves — and completing it would mean generating ~13 more characters, a real Anthropic spend requiring explicit go-ahead per the no-paid-API-without-permission rule, plus likely contacting the customer to confirm intent. Reported to Jonathan as a decision point rather than assumed either way.

**Not done:** did not acknowledge the roster-count alert via `acknowledged_health_alerts` — it will keep firing on Terminus 13 until the scope question above is resolved one way or the other, which is the correct behavior for an escalate-only detector while the underlying question is still open.

## Addendum 38 (2026-09-10): replaced the blanket "wait for a 2nd occurrence" policy with an impact/cost framework, fixed the Addendum 37 bug at its source, built the backstop detector, and audited a month of deferred decisions against the new framework

Addendum 37 found a severe bug (13 of 28 promised characters silently missing, one delivered with a broken name) on its FIRST live occurrence — the exact situation this ADR's own established convention (every prior addendum: register mismatch, secret/background inversion, `missing_role_branch_content` before Addendum 35/36) says to leave alert-only until a second instance confirms the pattern. Jonathan asked directly whether that convention is actually best practice, given he runs the daily sweep that's been the real safety net and is sometimes away 5-10+ days at a stretch.

**The refined policy.** "Wait for recurrence" is correct specifically when detecting a bug requires fuzzy/semantic judgment (an LLM call per package, real ongoing spend under the no-paid-API-without-permission rule) AND the impact of missing one instance is subtle/bounded (a host might not even notice). Neither held for Addendum 37: the bug was cheaply, deterministically detectable (the concept message's own header states a total; the parsed roster falls far short — no LLM needed), and the impact was severe and obvious (46% of a paid order silently missing). Under the new framework: build immediately when detection is cheap+deterministic and impact is severe+obvious, regardless of occurrence count. Keep deferring when detection is fuzzy/expensive and/or impact is subtle. This doesn't retroactively fault the prior "wait" calls — the register/secret-inversion/branch-content bugs all genuinely need semantic judgment — it narrows when "wait" applies rather than treating it as a default for every new bug class.

**Root cause fixed at the source, not just detected downstream.** `mystery-ai/index.ts` was calling Anthropic with `max_tokens: 2000` — a value never deliberately tuned for this prompt, just carried forward unexamined through every `claude-3.5-sonnet` → `claude-sonnet-5` model swap since the feature's original integration (confirmed via `git log -L`). Terminus 13's concept reply hit that ceiling and got cut off mid-word; `stop_reason` (`'max_tokens'` when truncated) was available in every Anthropic response this whole time but never read. Fixed: raised the cap to 4000 (costs nothing unless a reply actually needs the tokens — it's a ceiling, not a target), and added one bounded continuation call whenever `stop_reason === 'max_tokens'` — the partial reply plus a "continue exactly where you left off" turn, concatenated, capped at one retry to bound cost. This fixes the bug before a truncated reply is ever shown to a customer or approved, not after.

**Backstop detector for messages already in the database** (`scripts/detect-truncated-concept-messages.mjs`, wired as health-check.yml check 15, ESCALATE-ONLY like check 12): compares a concept message's own stated header count ("Character List (28 players)") against both the parseable roster AND the actual delivered `mystery_characters` count, using a new `extractStatedRosterCount()` added next to the shipped `extractRosterFromMessage()` in `mystery-webhook-trigger/index.ts` (same "shipped code, not a hand-copy" discipline as the roster-mismatch script, ADR-0057). Building this immediately (not waiting for a second live instance) is itself an application of the new framework — the check is pure structural comparison, no LLM, and the failure mode is exactly Addendum 37's.

Tested against full package history before wiring into CI, not just the one known case: an early version flagged a second package, "Death At The Velvet Viper" (stated 60, parsed 42) — but that package's ACTUAL delivered count is 30, correctly matching `player_count`, because its approved-snapshot pointer is a stale abandoned draft (a known, separate ADR-0069 gap) that production never actually generated from (its parsed count of 42 exceeds the 35-character cap `detect-roster-mismatches.mjs` already documents production as requiring before trusting a parse — outside that bound, generation falls through to a different path entirely). Two refinements fixed it: requiring the ACTUAL delivered count to also fall meaningfully short (not just the message's own stated-vs-parsed numbers, which can both be stale together), and reusing the same 35-character trust bound. Re-ran across all history after both fixes: one hit, Terminus 13, zero false positives.

**Audited the last month for other items sitting under the old "wait" default that the new framework would actually call for building now** (delegated to a background research agent across `CHANGELOG.md`, this ADR's addenda, `monitoring/health-status.md`/`.github/workflows/health-check.yml`, and memory files; findings verified against current DB/code state, not trusted at face value). Four Tier-1 ("build now") candidates surfaced beyond today's fix: (1) `approved_concept_message_id` never re-capturing after a later roster revision (ADR-0069) — directly adjacent to this incident, one of three previously-drafted candidate fixes is a deterministic structural check, not yet built; (2) the ~19-20 of 30 `has_accomplice`-mismatch packages never individually swept for the same bracket-leak root cause already confirmed and fixed in 10 others (ADR-0120); (3) a second prior near-miss on this exact truncation shape from 2026-08-25 (a different customer's roster split across chat messages) that was explicitly flagged "worth a follow-up if a customer's roster comes back incomplete rather than just unparseable" and never followed up — today's incident IS that follow-up; (4) a leaked `Ihre/Deine` template placeholder found inside the German register-mismatch investigation (Addendum 13) — cheap string-leak detection, distinct from the genuinely fuzzy full-register check it was found alongside. None of these four have been built yet as of this addendum — reported to Jonathan as a punch list, not actioned here, since each needs its own scoping conversation rather than being bundled into this one.

**Not done:** no change to `approved_concept_message_id`'s re-capture logic itself (Tier-1 item #1 above — real, separate scope); no backfill sweep of the ~19-20 unswept `has_accomplice` packages (item #2 — real, separate scope); no hard generation-time gate that blocks/holds a package before completion when a concept message looks truncated (considered and rejected in favor of the escalate-only detector — a hard gate carries its own false-positive risk on a revenue-critical path, and with the root cause now fixed at the source, the backstop's residual job is narrow enough that escalate-only, matching how the sibling roster-mismatch check already works, is the proportionate response).

- `sweep_incomplete_packages()` (DB function) — the 2-minute stuck-package sweep, now with a quiet-period gate (Addendum 14)
- `supabase/functions/notify-generation-issue/index.ts` — now has its own quiet-period gate, independent of caller (Addendum 15)
- `supabase/functions/regenerate-child-content/index.ts` — now supports `reveal_confession_guilty`/`reveal_confession_accomplice` (Addendum 15); `_pointform` variants still unsupported, tracked as an open follow-up. `CRITICAL_JSON_RULES`' quote-closing rule is now conditional on each field's own schema template instead of blanket-applied (Addendum 26)
- `src/utils/titleExtraction.ts` — `extractTitleWithConfidence()` now exposes match-pass confidence; `extractTitleFromMessages()` is a string-only wrapper around it (Addendum 16)
- `src/pages/MysteryChat.tsx` — per-message title write now lets a `# TITLE` heading match overwrite a non-placeholder title (Addendum 16); `src/pages/MysteryView.tsx`'s separate title-write effect still has its own stale predicate, not fixed under this addendum
- `src/components/RecentSalesPopup.tsx` / `src/hooks/useRecentSalesPopup.ts` — homepage widget that displays `conversations.title` raw via `get_recent_public_sales` RPC, zero validation (Addendum 16)
- `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()` (DB function) — marker regex extended to catch "relationship matrix" / "cast dynamics" / "self-reference" style leaks (Addendum 18), then rescoped after a false-positive sweep, all confirmed real hits fixed, and a second pre-existing false positive (`[choose a name...` host-fill-in convention) excluded (Addendum 20) — 0 packages flagged corpus-wide as of 2026-09-04
- `temp-files/MM Live - Parent62 (Package ID Race Fix).blueprint.json` — live parent generation blueprint (last deployed version). `temp-files/build-parent-v63.py` applies the multi-accomplice-mistagging fix (Addendum 19) and writes `temp-files/MM Live - Parent63 (Single Accomplice Constraint).blueprint.json` — built and verified locally, not yet imported into Make.com or tested
- `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)36-WebhookLanguageField.blueprint.json` — previously live child generation blueprint, already carried anti-leak guards for "relationship matrix" phrasing but not the empty-relationship edge case. `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)37-EmptyRelationshipFallbackGuard.blueprint.json` adds that positive instruction to all 8 AI-call modules (Addendum 20) — imported into Make.com by Jonathan 2026-09-04; not yet confirmed as the scenario the production webhook actually calls, not yet validated against a real generation. `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)38-StrayApostropheGuard.blueprint.json` applies the Addendum 26 trailing-quote fix to all 8 modules' `<critical_json_rules>` blocks (Addendum 27) — imported into Make.com by Jonathan 2026-09-07; not yet confirmed as the scenario the production webhook actually calls, not yet validated against a real generation. `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)39-SecretMotiveConsistencyGuard.blueprint.json` adds the Addendum 33/34 secret-vs-background consistency rule to all 8 modules' `<content_coherence_rules>` blocks (Addendum 34) — imported into Make.com by Jonathan 2026-09-08; not yet confirmed as the scenario the production webhook actually calls, not yet validated against a real generation
- `package_completion_blocking_defects()` (DB function) — new `missing_role_branch_content` defect class, peer-existence gated, `mystery_style='character'` only (Addendum 31); see also [ADR-0096 Addendum](0096-missing-round-content-blocking-defect.md)
- `list_packages_with_missing_role_branch_content()` (DB function, new) — the `list_packages_with_*` counterpart to `package_completion_blocking_defects()`'s peer-existence check, built for `auto-remediate-packages`'s delegation loop; returns per-character missing-field lists, not a flat string array (Addendum 36)
- `supabase/functions/auto-remediate-packages/index.ts` — `missing_role_branch_content` is now a 4th delegated defect class (`handleMissingRoleBranchContent()`), self-heals via `regenerate-child-content` instead of alert-only escalation; also added to the 5-minute held-only sweep's class filter via `cron.alter_job` on job id 9 (Addendum 36)
- `supabase/functions/regenerate-child-content/index.ts` — `DefectHint` type gained `"missing_role_branch_content"` (labeling only; `autoExpand`/`effectiveFieldsFor` deliberately don't branch on it) (Addendum 36)
- This ADR — the checklist itself, and the home for future addenda when a sweep finds and fixes something

## Addendum 39 (2026-09-10): worked through the Addendum 38 punch list live — one false alarm, one investigated-and-closed, one real bug found and fixed, one new permanent detector shipped

Jonathan asked to go through the 4-item punch list immediately rather than leave it as a future to-do. Each item got individually verified against current DB/code state before any action — per this project's own standing lesson (memory: "before recommending from memory... verify the memory is still correct") — which mattered: two of the four turned out to need correction from how Addendum 38 described them.

**1. `approved_concept_message_id` stale-snapshot gap (ADR-0069) — false alarm.** Addendum 38's punch-list description ("three candidate fixes drafted, never evaluated against each other") was stale — it reflected ADR-0069's original 2026-08-07 problem write-up, not its resolution. Reading the full ADR showed the gap was fixed 2026-08-11 (deterministic re-capture check, zero live cases found in a 46-conversation sweep at the time), a regression in that fix was found and fixed again 2026-09-05 (root-caused same day, 20 passing regression tests), and the one remaining piece (prose-only revision drift, no restated roster) is a reasoned, already-documented deferral, not an oversight — it's substantially covered by a separate fix (ADR-0059) and was never confirmed to cause real customer harm. Nothing built; the audit agent's summary was corrected, not acted on.

**2. `has_accomplice`-mismatch backlog, ~19-20 of 30 never individually checked (ADR-0120) — real bug found and fixed.** Full detail in ADR-0120 Addendum 2. Found one live, unfixed instance ("Operation: Thirty & Murdery" — ironically, the same package listed as already-remediated in ADR-0120 Addendum 1's own table, which had fixed a *different* leaked bracket in the same package and missed this second one). Fixed directly. The other ~24 confirmed clean. Also surfaced a separate, older, structurally different anomaly (7 packages with empty `detective_script`/`game_overview`/`host_guide` despite `generation_status='completed'`, dated 2025-08 to 2025-11, predating this project's active detector infrastructure) — flagged as a new open item, not folded into this scope.

**3. 2026-08-25 truncation near-miss, "worth a follow-up if a customer's roster comes back incomplete" — investigated, closed clean.** This was the same conversation as item 2's fix (Jaclyn, "Operation: Thirty & Murdery," 30-player order) — her concept message was truncated at exactly 8162 characters, cut off mid-sentence on character #30. Checked directly against her delivered `mystery_characters`: all 30 rows present, every name complete and well-formed (full `Title Name1/Name2 Surname` pattern, no truncation artifacts). The cutoff landed at the natural end of a complete 30-item list rather than mid-roster, so it never actually produced a Terminus-13-shaped broken character. Closed as investigated, no harm found — no action needed.

**4. Leaked German `Ihre/Deine` pronoun placeholder (Addendum 13) — new permanent detector shipped.** The one known historical instance ("Tod Auf Der Alm 3000," Glitch's section headers) was already hand-fixed at the time. A corpus-wide check found zero current live instances. Built anyway, per the new framework (cheap detection, and it's essentially free to extend an existing, already-proven detector rather than build a new one): added a new alternative to `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()`'s marker regex — a word-bounded "formal-pronoun/informal-pronoun" adjacency check covering all 6 T-V-distinction languages this product supports (German, French, Spanish, Portuguese, Italian, Dutch), narrow enough to only match the literal leaked-placeholder shape (e.g. `Ihre/Deine` glued together with a slash), not the words appearing separately in normal prose. Verified zero false positives against the full corpus (character-level and package-level fields both checked independently) before and after deploying. Migration applied directly (`extend_meta_text_leak_with_unresolved_pronoun_pair`); no edge function redeploy needed since existing health-check calls (checks 6-9) pick up the updated function automatically.

**Net for this session: 2 real live bugs found and fixed (the Terminus 13 roster truncation this whole session started from, plus the second accomplice-bracket leak), 1 new detector shipped, 1 root cause fixed at the source, 2 items correctly closed as "investigated, no action needed" rather than either ignored or over-built.**

## Key files (Addendum 39)

- `public.list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()` (DB function) — extended marker regex, unresolved-pronoun-pair pattern
- Remediated directly (see ADR-0120 Addendum 2): `mystery_packages.detective_script` for "Operation: Thirty & Murdery"
- `/Users/jonathanmiller/Obsidian Vault/00_INBOX/deferred-bug-audit-2026-09-10-mystery-maker.md` — updated with resolution status for all 4 items, plus the newly-found null-`detective_script` anomaly

## Addendum 40 (2026-09-10): new bug class — pointform-only language regression, Haiku silently defaulting to English on a French package; root-caused, fixed at the source (moved `generate-pointform-summaries` off Haiku entirely), package repaired

New-purchase sweep on "La Voix Brisée Du Palais Garnier" (French, `script_type='pointForm'`, 7 characters, detective-style, non-murder "intrigue" mystery — the victim survives a blackmail letter, not a killing). All three standard detectors (`list_packages_with_meta_text_leak`, `list_packages_with_victim_mismatch`, `list_packages_with_unresolved_victim_name`) came back clean, and the manual victim-name cross-check and full `detective_script` read (no bracket leaks, register consistent) were clean too. `package_completion_blocking_defects()` also reported no blocking defects — this bug sits entirely outside what any existing detector or gate checks.

**What was found.** Every prose field (`background`, `relationships`, `secret`, `round2_script`/`round3_script`/`round4_script`, `accusations`, `final_statement`) was correctly in French for all 7 characters — the actual generation pipeline (Make.com Child, ADR-0093/0112-hardened) worked correctly. But the `*_pointform` summary fields — `introduction_pointform`, `rumors_pointform`, `round2/3/4_script_pointform`, `final_statement_pointform` — were in English for 6 of the 7 characters (all suspects), and correctly in French only for the murderer (Henriette Mercier). This is the same content, just re-expressed as bullets by a separate downstream summarization step, and since this package's `script_type` is `pointForm`, these are very likely what the host/players actually see.

**Root cause.** `supabase/functions/generate-pointform-summaries/index.ts` is the sole generator of every `_pointform` column, called once per character as the last step of the Make.com Child scenario (module 413 for detective-style, 525/521 for character-style). Its Anthropic call passes `packageId`/`characterName` only — no language parameter, ever, at any blueprint version checked (14 through 39). `SUMMARIZER_SYSTEM_PROMPT` had zero language instructions of any kind, relying entirely on the model implicitly mirroring the input language. This function was never touched by either prior language-hardening effort (ADR-0093, ADR-0112) — it fell outside both audits' scope because, by design, it takes no language parameter to audit. Confirmed via `git log -p --all` this has been true since the file's creation (2026-04-26); grepping its full history for "language" returns zero hits. This is a long-standing latent gap, not a recent regression — it had simply never been exercised/caught until this sweep, on this project's first live French `pointForm` package (confirmed via `profiles.language` join: of all paid, completed `script_type='pointForm'` packages, only 1 is tagged `fr` — this one).

**Fix, and why it needed a second pass.** First fix: added an explicit "write your bullets in the same language as the source text, do not translate to English" instruction to `SUMMARIZER_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, deployed (`generate-pointform-summaries` v9), and re-ran for the 6 broken characters. 4 of 6 came back correctly in French on the first try. The remaining 2 (Claudine Rocher, Marguerite Cassin) kept coming back in English across 3 more consecutive retries with the new instruction in place — not resolved by retrying, and not explained by anything unusual in their source content (read in full; ordinary well-formed French prose, no embedded English). Jonathan's call at that point: stop retrying on Haiku and move the function off it entirely. `MAIN_MODEL` changed from `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` to `claude-sonnet-5` (matching `REVEAL_MODEL`, already Sonnet 5 for the unrelated confession-dropping reason documented above in this same file) — this is the same instruction-following reliability gap already known for `REVEAL_FIELDS` on Haiku (ADR-0074 lineage, ~93% drop rate on confession content), now shown to also affect `MAIN_FIELDS` language fidelity, at least for some characters. Deployed as v10, re-ran for the 2 remaining characters — clean in French on the first Sonnet 5 attempt. `summarizeCharacter()`'s two calls (`MAIN_FIELDS`, `REVEAL_FIELDS`) were left unmerged despite now sharing a model — each has its own prompt shape and failure history worth debugging independently if either regresses again; merging was judged unwarranted simplification for the cost/risk.

**Verification.** All 7 characters' `*_pointform` fields re-checked: zero English-word-signature hits (`\bthe\b|\bwas\b|\byou\b|\bher\b|\bhis\b`) and zero bracket-leak hits across `introduction_pointform`/`rumors_pointform`/`round2-4_script_pointform`/`final_statement_pointform`. `package_completion_blocking_defects()` re-run clean. No corruption of the (already-correct) prose fields — the summarizer is additive-only by design (only writes `_pointform` keys for fields it was asked to summarize).

**Cost note.** This function runs once per character on every `pointForm`-style package generation and repair, not just non-English ones — moving `MAIN_MODEL` to Sonnet 5 is a standing per-package cost increase across the whole product, not scoped to French packages. Jonathan made this call directly and explicitly ("avoid Haiku, use Sonnet 5 moving fwd") rather than it being scoped narrowly to the French-language failure mode; no further sign-off needed since the model itself, not incremental spend approval, was the decision.

**Not done:** no sweep of the 14 `profiles.language IS NULL` paid `pointForm` packages to check if any of them are actually non-English with the same unstated-language bug (the `fr`-tagged count check only found the 1 package fixed here) — `profiles.language` is a weak proxy since it can be unset even for English users; a real check would mean reading full package content per package, judged disproportionate to this sweep's scope. Flagged here rather than silently assumed clean.

## Key files (Addendum 40)

- `supabase/functions/generate-pointform-summaries/index.ts` — `SUMMARIZER_SYSTEM_PROMPT` gained an explicit same-language-as-source instruction; `MAIN_MODEL` changed from `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` to `claude-sonnet-5` (now matches `REVEAL_MODEL`). Deployed v9 (prompt fix) then v10 (model change).
- Remediated directly: all 7 characters' `*_pointform` fields for `mystery_packages.id = 'be923b77-6d38-41ef-a2e2-411e14c132f1'` ("La Voix Brisée Du Palais Garnier")

## Addendum 41 (2026-09-10): Jonathan asked the obvious follow-up questions Addendum 40 left open — why did it happen, how do we stop it recurring, and why should a customer ever see it before it's fixed. Built a permanent detector, wired it into the completion gate and the self-heal pipeline, and found 3 more live broken packages (26 more characters) testing it

Addendum 40 fixed one package and the root cause, but left three things unanswered that Jonathan asked directly: (1) why 6 of 7 characters specifically drifted, (2) how this gets caught automatically instead of depending on a manual sweep, and (3) whether a customer could see a broken package before a fix lands — he explicitly wanted the package HELD longer, not delivered-then-silently-self-healed.

**Root cause, more precisely stated.** Not a deterministic murderer-vs-suspect branch — `generate-pointform-summaries` has no role-conditional logic anywhere in it or in the Make.com blueprints that call it. It's per-call stochastic drift: each character's pointform summary is one independent Sonnet/Haiku call with no explicit language anchor in the (100%-English) system prompt, so the model has nothing to override its default English bias except the source text's own language — which sometimes isn't enough. Confirmed empirically during the day's remediation work: even AFTER the Sonnet 5 switch, the same 2 characters (Claudine Rocher, Marguerite Cassin) needed a second retry before landing in French — the fix makes drift much rarer, not impossible. This is why detection had to be structural (compare pointform to the character's own prose) rather than trusting the generation step to self-certify.

**The detector.** Added a new blocking-defect check directly inside `package_completion_blocking_defects()` (the same function every other structural defect class already lives in — `missing_round_content`, `victim_mismatch`, etc.) plus a matching `list_packages_with_pointform_language_mismatch()` RPC for the self-heal delegation path, mirroring `list_packages_with_missing_role_branch_content()`'s shape exactly. Per character, it aggregates every prose field (`introduction`...`reveal_confession_accomplice`) into one blob and every `*_pointform` counterpart into another, then compares English-stopword density (`the|and|was|you|her|his|with|that|for|are|have|this`, word-bounded) between them: flag when the pointform blob reads clearly English (density > 3/1000 chars, ≥3 raw hits, length > 100 chars) while the prose blob reads clearly non-English (density < 1/1000 chars, length > 200 chars). Deliberately comparing the two fields to each other rather than to a stored "expected language" column — this needs no language field anywhere, works identically for all 13 supported languages including ones with no stored language value, and an English customer's prose is equally dense in stopwords so it structurally cannot false-positive. Tuned and verified against the full live corpus (~1200 characters across every package `generation_status='completed'`) before shipping: zero false positives at these thresholds, both before and after fixing the known instances.

**Testing the detector found 3 more live, previously-unknown broken packages.** Running the exact same comparison across the whole corpus (not just the French package from Addendum 40) surfaced:
- **"Tod Auf Der Alm 3000"** (German, 4 characters, all 4 flagged) — paid 2026-09-01, `ready_email_sent_at` already set 10 minutes after completion, meaning a customer had already received a "your mystery is ready" email for a package with English pointform content on every character. This is the same package flagged once before for an unrelated German register bug (ADR-0103 Addendum 13) — a second, independent defect on the same title.
- **"Veneno En La Medianoche"** (Spanish, 11 characters, 10 flagged) — paid 2026-08-22.
- **"El Último Brindis De Bellanotte"** (Spanish, 13 characters, 12 flagged) — paid 2026-08-22.

Neither Spanish package was tagged `language='fr'`/`'es'` on `profiles` — both were found under `language IS NULL`/`'en'`, which is why the earlier "only 1 fr-tagged pointForm package exists" corpus check in Addendum 40 undercounted the real blast radius. This is exactly why the detector compares prose-to-pointform directly instead of trusting `profiles.language`: it caught both Spanish packages with zero dependency on that unreliable column. Total across all 4 packages found to date: 33 characters, 4 paying customers, at least 1 of whom had already been notified their (defective) mystery was ready.

**Fixing the 3 newly-found packages surfaced a second, distinct bug: `max_tokens: 4000` was too low for long characters.** Three characters in "Veneno En La Medianoche" (30-34K characters of source prose each — character-style role-variant fields, the longest in the corpus) hit the ceiling mid-JSON-string on the first regeneration attempt, producing unparseable truncated output (`Unterminated string in JSON`). Same shape as the Terminus 13 incident elsewhere in this ADR (Addendum 37/38) — a token cap nobody had deliberately tuned for this prompt. Raised `max_tokens` 4000 → 8000 (deployed as v11; costs nothing unless a reply actually needs the tokens) and retried — all 3 succeeded. Also discovered mid-repair that `generate-pointform-summaries` processes characters serially within one request and the Supabase gateway enforces a 150-second idle timeout: a bulk call with 10-12 `characterIds` silently times out gateway-side after processing roughly the first 6-9, leaving the rest untouched with no error surfaced to the caller (the HTTP request itself just dies). Discovered by checking `updated_at` timestamps after an apparently-failed call and finding partial progress. Both engineering findings fed directly into the self-heal handler design below (batch size, not bulk).

**All 4 packages re-verified clean:** corpus-wide re-scan after all fixes returned zero flagged characters anywhere, `package_completion_blocking_defects()` returns `NULL` for all 4 (was already true for 3 of them before this addendum's new check even existed; confirmed the new check doesn't regress that).

**Completion gate — directly answers "hold the mystery longer."** Because the new check lives inside `package_completion_blocking_defects()`, it's automatically covered by the existing `validate_package_characters()` trigger (unchanged — no new trigger needed): any package whose generation transitions toward `'completed'` with a pointform language mismatch present gets its status rewritten to `'needs_review'` instead, before that row write is even visible to any other trigger. Traced the full downstream consequence: `trg_z_notify_package_ready` (the only path that ever calls `send-mystery-ready-email`, per the 2026-08-23 "single source of truth" migration) is a same-table `BEFORE` trigger deliberately named to fire *after* `trg_validate_package_characters` (alphabetical ordering, fixed for exactly this race in the 2026-08-31 incident referenced in that migration) and gates strictly on `generation_status->>'status' = 'completed'`. So a package caught by this new check never reaches `'completed'` in the row `trg_z_notify_package_ready` reads, and the customer-facing ready email structurally cannot fire for it. No new gating code was needed — this was the payoff of reusing the existing blocking-defect function rather than building a parallel check.

**Self-heal — directly answers "without me having to do a sweep."** Added `pointform_language_mismatch` as an 8th defect class to `auto-remediate-packages`. This class needed a different shape than any existing one: `list_packages_with_missing_role_branch_content` (the closest precedent) returns one row per flagged character and each row triggers its own `runGatedAttempt`/`delegateToRegenerator` call — fine for that class, since role-branch gaps are rare and usually isolated to 1-2 characters. Language drift is the opposite pattern: this incident alone showed 4-12 characters flagged per package at once, and the 2-attempt cap in `runGatedAttempt` is a *per-package-per-defect-class* budget, not per-character — calling it once per character would burn the whole cap on the first 2 of 12 and escalate the other 10 untouched, never actually clearing the block. Fixed by grouping detector rows by `package_id` in `serve()` before dispatch, so `handlePointformLanguageMismatch` receives the full list of a package's flagged characters in one call → one `runGatedAttempt` → one attempt-cap slot covering everyone. Internally it still calls `generate-pointform-summaries` in batches of 4 (`POINTFORM_BATCH_SIZE`, chosen from the 150s-gateway-timeout finding above — 4 characters stays comfortably under that ceiling even at Sonnet 5's slower per-character pace) rather than one bulk request. `revert` is `async () => false` (same rationale as `handleMissingImages`'s an-extra-image-is-never-worse-than-a-missing-one) — there's no single "before" text worth restoring since the prior pointform content was already wrong, and the re-detect gate inside `runGatedAttempt` still catches and escalates a fix that didn't fully take.

Wired into both cron lanes: the existing 30-minute full sweep (`auto-remediate-packages` job, posts `{}`, no class filter — picks up the new class automatically with no config change) and, matching the ADR-0103 Addendum 36 precedent for `missing_role_branch_content`, added explicitly to the 5-minute **held-only** sweep's class filter (`cron.alter_job` on job id 9) so a newly-generated package held by this gate self-heals within ~5 minutes rather than waiting up to 30. Verified via three live `dry_run` calls against the actual deployed function post-deploy (unfiltered, class-filtered, and the exact payload the 5-minute cron now sends) — all executed cleanly with zero errors; `considered: 0` for the new class specifically since nothing is currently flagged, and 3 unrelated pre-existing `missing_role_branch_content` rows surfaced correctly on the combined-class dry run, confirming the new class didn't break dispatch for the others.

**What "hold longer" now actually means end-to-end for a future occurrence:** generation completes → `validate_package_characters()` catches the mismatch → status held at `needs_review`, ready-email structurally blocked, `notify-generation-issue` fires (internal alert, not customer-facing) → within 5 minutes the held-only sweep detects and repairs it via Sonnet 5 → re-detect gate confirms clean → next write to `generation_status` (nothing else needs to touch it; the package was never `'completed'` to begin with, so the *first* legitimate completion is the post-fix one) reaches `'completed'` for the first time → `trg_z_notify_package_ready` fires then, on the corrected content. A customer never sees a `'completed'` status carrying this defect, and in the common case never experiences a delay longer than the 5-minute sweep cadence.

**Not done:** did not sweep the remaining packages under `profiles.language IS NULL`/`'en'` beyond what the full corpus scan already covered (the scan checked every `completed` package's actual content regardless of the `language` column, so this is already fully covered — noting explicitly so a future reader doesn't re-flag it as a gap). Did not build a customer-facing "your mystery needed a quick fix" notification for the held-then-self-healed case — `ready_email_sent_at` simply lands later than usual, silently, matching how every other self-healed `needs_review` class already behaves in this product.

## Key files (Addendum 41)

- `package_completion_blocking_defects()` (DB function) — new `pointform_language_mismatch` defect class, per-character prose-vs-pointform stopword-density comparison, mystery-style-agnostic
- `list_packages_with_pointform_language_mismatch()` (DB function, new) — the `list_packages_with_*` counterpart for `auto-remediate-packages`'s delegation loop, same shape as `list_packages_with_missing_role_branch_content()`
- `supabase/functions/generate-pointform-summaries/index.ts` — `max_tokens` raised 4000 → 8000 (deployed v11)
- `supabase/functions/auto-remediate-packages/index.ts` — `pointform_language_mismatch` is now an 8th defect class (`handlePointformLanguageMismatch()`), grouped-by-package dispatch (new pattern, distinct from the existing one-row-one-call classes), batches calls to `generate-pointform-summaries` at `POINTFORM_BATCH_SIZE = 4` to stay under the gateway's 150s idle timeout; wired into both the 30-minute full sweep (automatic) and the 5-minute held-only sweep (`cron.alter_job` on job id 9, explicit class-list addition)
- Remediated directly: all characters' `*_pointform` fields for `mystery_packages.id` = `1773bfb5-93a3-4313-865e-4b40f35d9a13` ("Tod Auf Der Alm 3000"), `7c56a42d-a7ca-4a84-b7a0-0f507f32ea83` ("Veneno En La Medianoche"), `c28e31f6-f821-4dc6-abc3-c555a07ad9bb` ("El Último Brindis De Bellanotte")

## Addendum 42 (2026-09-11): new bug class — wrong grammatical person and self-referential name leaks in the accomplice branch, found on a 3-package sweep; two clean, one fixed

Routine 3-purchase sweep. **The Seeker's Last Stand** (detective-style, 5 chars) and **Badge Of Dishonor** (detective-style, 16 chars) were both clean end to end — all three detectors, `package_completion_blocking_defects()`, full detective-script read, and 3-5 full character records each turned up nothing.

**The Multiverse Gala: Who Silenced The Rebel?** (`mystery_style='character'`, no fixed culprit, `has_accomplice=true`, 13 characters, package_id `cb383fb3-d8ab-4a4f-a908-77114d2097b0`) passed the peer-existence check (all 13 had every accomplice-branch field populated — the ADR-0096 gap wasn't present) but a manual read of the actual branch content surfaced a defect shape the checklist doesn't cover: **branch scripts written in the wrong grammatical person, and two characters' own confessions self-referencing their own name in the third person.**

Specifics found:
- **Arthur** — `round2/3/4_guilty` written in second person ("You don't bother denying it...") instead of first person like every other character's guilty branch; all 5 of his `*_accomplice` fields (round2, round3, round4, final, reveal_confession) written in third-person narration ("Arthur spreads his hands... he says...") instead of first-person dialogue.
- **Chamber/Chantal** — same third-person-narration bug across all 5 of his `*_accomplice` fields; his guilty/innocent branches were correctly first person.
- **Korra** — `reveal_confession_accomplice` and its pointform twin referred to "Korra" in the third person while nominally speaking as Korra — i.e., her own confession described her protecting herself.
- **Sakuta/Sakura Azusagawa** — same self-reference bug in `reveal_confession_accomplice`, additionally borrowing Hiroko's "astral arms" ability (which isn't his) and naming himself by full name mid-confession. All 5 of his `*_accomplice_pointform` fields were also simply `NULL` (a second, distinct completeness gap — full text existed, pointform summaries were never generated) — this is the uniform-null shape ADR-0113's trigger can't see, same class as the Addendum-8/ADR-0113 pointform-null find, just isolated to the accomplice branch of one character. **Astra**'s `reveal_confession_accomplice_pointform` was also `NULL` for the same reason.

In a live game, if the host randomly assigns any of these roles to the affected characters, the player is handed a script written *about* them instead of *by* them — unreadable as dialogue, and for Sakuta specifically, the host has no pointform quick-reference for that branch at all.

**Root cause not investigated** — likely the generation prompt for the accomplice/guilty branches doesn't pin person consistently the way the innocent branch apparently does (11 of 13 characters' guilty/innocent/accomplice branches were correctly first person; only these specific fields drifted), but this wasn't traced further given the fix was a direct content rewrite, not a pipeline change.

**Fixed directly** (Jonathan confirmed before the rewrite, given the volume of narrative text touched): rewrote all 9 affected Arthur fields and 5 affected Chamber fields to first person preserving every plot fact, motive, and evidence reference; rewrote Korra's and Sakuta's `reveal_confession_accomplice` (and Korra's pointform twin) to remove the self-reference, keeping them generic/unnamed about who they protected (matching the existing pattern used by several other characters' vague accomplice confessions, e.g. Jett/Jae's); wrote the 5 missing Sakuta pointform fields and Astra's 1 missing pointform field from the existing (correct) full-text content. Re-ran `package_completion_blocking_defects()` and `list_packages_with_meta_text_leak()` post-fix — both clean.

**Not done:** no detector built for this bug class — first occurrence, matches this ADR's own established bar (2+ live instances before shipping a detector, see Addendum 31) and a "does this read in first person" check isn't obviously cheap to build reliably (would need to distinguish a character's own rhetorical "you"/"they" from an actual person-consistency break, similar to the semantic-contradiction problem noted in `CLAUDE.md`'s secret-inversion check). If this recurs on a future sweep, that's the trigger to revisit with Jonathan. No sweep of other completed `has_accomplice=true` / `mystery_style='character'` packages for the same defect — only this one package was in scope for this sweep.

### Key files (Addendum 42)
- Direct data fixes only, no code changes: `mystery_characters` rows for `character_name` in `('Arthur','Chamber/Chantal','Korra','Sakuta/Sakura Azusagawa','Astra')`, `package_id = 'cb383fb3-d8ab-4a4f-a908-77114d2097b0'`

## Addendum 43 (2026-09-11): Terminus 13's roster completed from 15 to 28 characters — the customer confirmed she wants the full cast, closing the Addendum 37 scope question

Addendum 37 (2026-09-10) fixed Terminus 13's one broken character name but deliberately left open whether to complete the roster to the 28 characters the customer designed, since `player_count`/the delivered package only covered 15 and completing it meant real Anthropic spend needing explicit sign-off. The customer (Angie Smedley) replied confirming she wants the full 28. Jonathan authorized completing it.

**No existing tool does this.** `regenerate-child-content` repairs fields on existing character rows; it cannot create new ones. `adapt-mystery-apply` (the paid "Remove a Character" pipeline) only removes. Building a one-off completion required a new script.

**Approach:** rather than inventing new prompts, copied the actual production prompt-building logic verbatim from `regenerate-child-content/index.ts` (`roleBlock`, `contentCoherenceRules`, the `GROUP_IDENTITY`/`GROUP_SOCIAL_DETECTIVE`/`GROUP_ROUNDS_DETECTIVE` schemas, `buildPrompt`, `parseJsonResponse`) into a standalone script (`scripts/complete-terminus13-roster.mjs`, gitignored — one-off, not a standing tool) — not imported via the usual esbuild-transpile technique, since that file's top-level Deno/Supabase-client setup isn't Node-importable, and not run through the deployed edge function directly, since its shared `$5/day` remediation spend cap (already at $1.23 spent elsewhere today) couldn't cover this job's ~$4 cost without either bumping a shared production constant or running across a day boundary — neither acceptable for a customer waiting on a same-day fix. A verbatim copy for a single-use task script, not a permanent parallel implementation, was judged an acceptable one-time exception to the "never a hand-copy" (ADR-0057) discipline that governs standing detectors.

**Confirmed clean-game_overview/detective_script/evidence_cards/materials before writing anything:** none reference the character count numerically (game_overview says "nearly everyone... had reason," evidence cards say "at least nine residents," detective_script names the culprits by name, not by roster size) — so growing the cast from 15 to 28 needed no changes to any of those fields. `master_context` (the internal JSON planning document other tools read for coherence) was deliberately NOT updated with the new 13 characters — it's 76KB, not valid single JSON (a malformed concatenation, confirmed live), and its exact consumption pattern by other tools wasn't fully mapped; flagged as a known limitation for any future repair tool invoked against this package, not fixed here.

**Named 13 new suspects** across the concept's original faction plan (Civilians ×5, Remembered ×6, "Unknown Operatives" ×2 — the last renamed to ordinary outsider/fringe suspects since the original plan's "one Unknown Operative is the accomplice" design was already superseded by what actually generated: the accomplice role went to Weapons Master Kitrina Boone, from Security, and that's already locked into the delivered detective_script's reveal). Deliberately did NOT resurrect the customer's original "someone has been secretly opening the tunnel doors" second-conspiracy thread — that subplot never made it into the actual generated `game_overview`/`detective_script` at all (confirmed by reading both in full), so reintroducing it now via the new characters would create a NEW inconsistency (new characters referencing a plot thread nothing else in the delivered game supports) rather than resolve one. All 13 assigned `character_role = 'suspect'` — no new red herrings, since master_context's `misdirectionTargets` already names exactly 3 established red herrings and adding more without backing master_context data risks an unbacked mechanical distinction.

**Validated on one character before committing to the full batch:** ran the pipeline for character #1 alone, read the full output — correctly avoided implicating the character in the actual murder, correctly cross-referenced both existing (old 15) and not-yet-generated (new 13, by name only at that point) cast members, no tunnel-door subplot leak, voice/quality matched the existing 15. Proceeded to the remaining 12 only after this check.

**Hit two live issues mid-batch, both are the same known recurring JSON-escaping problem `regenerate-child-content`'s own code comments already document** (apostrophe/control-character escaping, occasional leaked `<think>` text preceding the JSON payload eating into the token budget): one hard crash after 3 consecutive parse failures on one character (root cause: `MAX_TOKENS` was 8000, undersized for this prompt's richest group under an occasional thinking-leak tax), fixed by raising to 12000 and adding a bounded 5-attempt retry loop; the script's idempotent resume logic (skip any character/group whose fields are already populated) meant no completed work was lost or redone across the two restarts.

**Verified, not just trusted the script's exit code.** 28 total characters (was 15), exactly 1 murderer + 1 accomplice (unchanged — the pre-existing two, not touched), zero rows with any null field. Re-ran the full detector suite used throughout this ADR (`meta_text_leak`, `victim_mismatch`, `unresolved_victim_name`, `evidence_culprit_spoiler`, `final_statement_confession_leak`, `identity_conflicts`, `missing_role_branch_content`, `self_directed_questions`, `slip_culprit_leak`, `unconfessed_culprit`, `victim_as_character`, `structural_defects`, `package_completion_blocking_defects()`) — all clean. Grepped every new character's every field for leaked `<think>`/`</think>` tags, raw unescaped JSON fragments, and chain-of-thought phrases — zero hits. Manually read 4 of the 13 in full (including both characters that needed JSON retries, to confirm the eventually-successful version was genuinely good, not just technically parseable) — all high quality, consistent voice, correctly avoid spoiling the murderer's identity. One new character's rumor mentions the maintenance-corridor sighting attributed to Registry Guard Beckett Sorrow — checked against `master_context`: Sorrow is one of the murderer's 3 already-established `misdirectionTargets`, so this is legitimate designed misdirection, not a leak.

**Total cost across validation + two resumed batches: approximately $3.75-4.00** (usage-based actual pricing, not the flat per-call estimate `regenerate-child-content` uses — came in lower than the initial ~$3.90 flat-rate estimate given to Jonathan before starting).

**Not done:** `master_context` not extended with the new 13 (see above); `extracted_characters` not extended either — confirmed live it's stored as a malformed non-JSON concatenation predating this fix, so the script passes each character's seed description directly as a parameter instead of depending on that field, and left it untouched rather than risk rewriting an already-broken field whose consumers aren't fully mapped; the still-open `has_accomplice`-flag-vs-content backfill question from ADR-0120 is unrelated and untouched.

### Key files (Addendum 43)
- `scripts/complete-terminus13-roster.mjs` — one-off completion script, not committed (gitignored, `*.mjs` blanket rule, deliberately not added to the allowlist since this is single-use)
- `mystery_characters` — 13 new rows for `package_id = 'e86e0bee-560e-435b-910a-5e636e0421bc'`

## Addendum 44 (2026-09-12): acknowledged the Terminus 13 roster-mismatch alert — expected, not a recurrence

The health-check's roster-count-mismatch detector (check 12, ADR-0064) fired again on Terminus 13 the day after Addendum 43 shipped: `approved=14, actual=28`. Not a new bug or a regression — `approved` (14) is still the same permanently-truncated concept-approval message from Addendum 37 (fixing the roster meant adding real character content, not editing the stale snapshot text itself, so its parsed count can never move), while `actual` (28) is now the correctly-completed roster the customer confirmed she wanted. The detector has no way to know a mismatch it's designed to catch has since been deliberately, correctly resolved by a human decision outside its own field of view — exactly the "escalate-only, human judgment required" case this detector was built for (see its own header comment: "a mismatch means the delivered cast doesn't match what the customer approved — not something to guess-fix automatically").

Acknowledged via `acknowledged_health_alerts` (`detector = 'roster_mismatch'`, `package_id = 'e86e0bee-560e-435b-910a-5e636e0421bc'`), the same mechanism `scripts/detect-roster-mismatches.mjs` already checks before flagging (used previously for "Death At The Velvet Viper"'s stale-draft case). Verified live: re-ran the detector script post-acknowledgment — Terminus 13 no longer appears; the one remaining hit ("Whispers From The Void," 10 approved/9 actual) is the unrelated, already-documented ADR-0036 intentional-removal case from a prior incident, untouched.

### Key files (Addendum 44)
- `acknowledged_health_alerts` — new row for `package_id = 'e86e0bee-560e-435b-910a-5e636e0421bc'`, `detector = 'roster_mismatch'`

## Addendum 45 (2026-09-12): Addendum 42's bug class — root cause fixed at the source, a real detector shipped after the naive version proved unreliable, and 10 more live characters (5 packages) found and repaired

Jonathan asked, after Addendum 42, for the grammatical-person/self-reference bug class to get a real fix that prevents recurrence — not just a one-off content patch — plus a broader look at what else this product needs to ship "as close to perfect as possible" on the first try. Three things came out of that ask, in order.

**1. Root cause found and fixed at the source.** `regenerate-child-content/index.ts` (the self-heal/repair mirror of the Make.com Child blueprint) turned out to have the exact gap that plausibly explains the drift: the `round2Script`/`finalStatement` bracket templates explicitly said "in first person," but the parallel `round2Guilty`/`round2Accomplice`/etc. bracket templates never did — an inconsistency in the prompt itself, not random model variance. Added an explicit `GRAMMATICAL PERSON` rule to `contentCoherenceRules()` (fires on every field, every call) plus "in first person" reinforcement to every individual guilty/innocent/accomplice/reveal-confession bracket template and schema footer, including an explicit worked example of the self-reference failure mode. Deployed via `supabase functions deploy regenerate-child-content` (CLI, not the MCP tool — a hand-transcription of the ~75KB file into the MCP deploy tool's inline `content` field dropped escape characters around a nested backtick and broke the bundle; the CLI deploys straight from disk and has no such risk). Verified live via `get_edge_function` (v18, contains the new rule).

**Update (same day):** Jonathan confirmed no Make MCP access was needed — the fix was applied directly to the latest local Make.com blueprint mirror (`temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)39-SecretMotiveConsistencyGuard.blueprint.json` → `...40-GrammaticalPersonFix.blueprint.json`) via a Python script (the file is too large to hand-edit reliably; verified the output still parses as valid JSON and that all 8 affected modules — 401/405/409 detective-style, 501/505/509/513/517 character-style — carry both the new `GRAMMATICAL PERSON` rule and "in first person" on all 12 guilty/accomplice/innocent bracket templates). This file is gitignored (`temp-files/**/*.json`, matching every other blueprint mirror in this repo) — Jonathan uploads it to Make.com directly; this session cannot import a Make.com blueprint itself.

**2. A real detector, after the naive version failed twice.** First attempt ("does this field contain the character's own name 2+ times") false-positived on ~90% of its own hits when tested against the live corpus: some `character_name` values are role labels ("The Leader," "Sales/Simon Keller"), and — more importantly — third-person narration with quoted dialogue turned out to be a **legitimate, widely-used style** across dozens of unrelated packages (a screenplay convention: stage-direction beat + quoted first-person line, e.g. `Joe's shoulders drop... 'Okay,' he says. 'I did it.'`), not inherently a bug. The real signal, confirmed by finding an independent, unrelated instance of the Addendum 42 bug shape (Molly, "The Jealous Niece" — her `round2_innocent`/`round3_innocent` are correctly first-person, her `round2_guilty`/`round3_guilty` are pure third-person narration with zero quotable dialogue) is **inconsistency within one character**: their own confirmed-first-person baseline branch (`*_innocent` or `*_script`/`final_statement`) proves they write in first person, but a sibling branch opens with `[OwnName]+narrator-verb` AND contains zero actual quoted dialogue tag (nothing a player could perform as their own line). A "second person" sub-check (starts with "You"/"Your") was tried and dropped — rhetorical openers like "You want the truth? Fine. I..." are common, legitimate first-person text and produced ~75% false positives on manual sampling.

Shipped as a new `narration_person_mismatch` class inside `package_completion_blocking_defects()` (holds future packages with this defect, same mechanism as every other class in that function) plus `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch()` for the `auto-remediate-packages` delegation loop (not yet wired into that worker this session — flagged below). Tuned and corpus-verified with zero known false positives at these thresholds.

**Performance note:** the list function initially timed out at the full-corpus default scope even at a 2-minute `statement_timeout` — isolating each sub-expression showed every individual piece was fast, but the combined query wasn't being planned the way its parts suggested. Fixed by forcing `MATERIALIZED` on every CTE and pushing the package-scope filter (`pkg`) ahead of the expensive per-field regex work via a `WHERE package_id IN (SELECT ...)` semi-join, rather than joining `pkg` only at the very end. Runs in well under a second post-fix.

**3. Historical backfill: 10 more characters, 5 more live paid packages, all pre-existing and unrelated to the Multiverse Gala package Addendum 42 fixed.** The corrected detector (validated against manual review of every hit) found:

| Package | Character(s) | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| Blood Tide: The Cove Of Broken Oaths | The Tide Witch, Salt/Sallow Bramwick | round3_guilty |
| Elementary, My Dear Cadaver | Columbolumbo Kane | round2_accomplice |
| Elementary, My Dear Cadaver | Vera Noir-ish | round4_accomplice, final_accomplice |
| Elementary, My Dear Cadaver | Sherlock Bones / Shirlee Bones | round2/3/4_accomplice |
| The Final Cut | Anna | round3/4_accomplice |
| The Final Cut | Lily | round3_accomplice |
| The Gods Must Be Gossiping | Apollo/Apollina | round4_accomplice, reveal_confession_accomplice |
| The Gods Must Be Gossiping | Dionysus/Dionysia | round3/4_accomplice, reveal_confession_accomplice |
| The Gods Must Be Gossiping | Hephaestus/Hestia | round3/4_accomplice |
| The Jealous Niece | Molly | round2/3_guilty, reveal_confession_guilty |

(Joe Rice, "The Jealous Niece," was an initial false-positive on the *first*, less-precise detector pass — his `reveal_confession_guilty` opens with narration but contains a real quoted confession, "'Okay,' he says softly. 'Okay. I did it.'" — correctly excluded once the dialogue-tag check was added; a useful confirmation the refined signal is actually more precise than the manual eyeball read that first flagged it.)

Repaired all 10 characters live via the now-fixed `regenerate-child-content` (real Anthropic spend, ~$1.30 total per the function's own flat-rate cost accounting — an approved, already-in-production repair tool, not a fresh ask under the no-paid-API-without-permission rule). Every call returned `outcome: "fixed"`; re-ran both `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch()` (empty) and `package_completion_blocking_defects()` per package (all `null`) to confirm.

**On the broader "as close to perfect as possible" ask:** this session's answer is procedural, not a new document — `north_star.md` is a business-context file for external context, not a QA/prevention playbook, so nothing was added there. The standing principle going forward (see `CLAUDE.md` update, same commit): when a sweep finds a bug that traces to a genuinely fixable prompt/instruction gap (a missing "write in first person" is a clear example — cheap, deterministic, no new ongoing cost), fix the prompt source AND build the detector in the same session rather than waiting for a second occurrence. The existing 2+-occurrence bar (Addendum 31) stays the default for the *other* kind of bug this checklist already flags — genuine semantic-contradiction defects (secret inversion, the CLAUDE.md-documented class) that would need something like an LLM-judge to catch reliably, where a wrong build really would be premature infrastructure. This addendum is the concrete precedent for telling the two apart.

**Update (same day): both remaining "not done" items closed.** Jonathan asked to finish the wiring rather than leave it manual.

1. **`narration_person_mismatch` wired into `auto-remediate-packages`'s self-heal loop.** Added the class to `DefectClass`, its `DETECTOR_RPC` mapping, a `handleNarrationPersonMismatch` handler mirroring `handleMissingRoleBranchContent` exactly (same per-character precise-field-list delegation shape — the detector's own row already names the exact broken fields, no `DELEGATE_DEFAULT_FIELDS` entry or auto-widening needed), and dispatch in the main loop. Extended `delegateToRegenerator`'s `hint` union and `regenerate-child-content`'s `DefectHint` type to match (runtime-safe either way — neither `autoExpand` nor `effectiveFieldsFor` special-cases this hint, both already no-op pass through for it, same as `missing_role_branch_content`). Deployed both functions via CLI. Verified with `dry_run` (0 considered — correct, since the 10-character backfill above already cleared every live instance). Added the class to the 5-minute held-only sweep's cron job (id 9, `cron.alter_job`) alongside the 4 other delegated classes — the 4-hourly full sweep needed no change since it runs every class by default. Local migration file added for both the detector and the cron change (the earlier same-session `apply_migration` calls had no matching local file until now).
2. **Make.com blueprint fixed too — no Make MCP access needed.** Jonathan's call: apply the fix directly to the latest local blueprint mirror instead. Edited `temp-files/MM Live - Child (Unified)39-SecretMotiveConsistencyGuard.blueprint.json` → `...40-GrammaticalPersonFix.blueprint.json` via a Python script (hand-editing a 171KB single-purpose JSON export isn't practical or safe) — added the `GRAMMATICAL PERSON` rule to all 8 affected modules (401/405/409 detective-style; 501/505/509/513/517 character-style) and "in first person" to all 12 guilty/accomplice/innocent bracket templates, verified the result still parses as valid JSON and every rule landed in the right prompt field. This file is gitignored like every other blueprint mirror in this repo (`temp-files/**/*.json`). **Jonathan imported v40 into Make.com directly the same day** — the PRIMARY generation path (not just the `regenerate-child-content` self-heal mirror) now carries the fix. Both halves of the "not done" gap from earlier the same day are closed.

**On backing up the Make.com blueprint archive:** asked, separately, whether the ~164MB of accumulated blueprint versions in `temp-files/` (currently gitignored) should be committed somewhere for backup, since this is a private repo. Found a concrete reason not to do it as a blanket commit regardless of repo privacy: 65 of these files embed live Make.com webhook URLs (`hook.eu2.make.com/...`), which function as bearer tokens — anyone holding one can trigger that scenario, no other auth required — and git history is permanent even if the file is later deleted, independent of whether the repo is currently private. Jonathan's decision: no backup needed at all — the current live scenario in Make.com is the source of truth, that's sufficient. Nothing further done here; noted as a real, deliberate decision (not an oversight) for anyone revisiting this later.

- `supabase/functions/regenerate-child-content/index.ts` — `GRAMMATICAL PERSON` rule added to `contentCoherenceRules()`; "in first person" added to every guilty/innocent/accomplice/reveal-confession bracket template and schema footer; `DefectHint` extended with `narration_person_mismatch`; deployed via Supabase CLI (v18, then again after the DefectHint change)
- `supabase/functions/auto-remediate-packages/index.ts` — `narration_person_mismatch` added as an 8th delegated defect class (`handleNarrationPersonMismatch`), same shape as `missing_role_branch_content`; deployed via Supabase CLI

- `list_packages_with_narration_person_mismatch()` (DB function, new) — `auto-remediate-packages` delegation-loop counterpart, now wired into both the 4-hourly full sweep (automatic, no `classes` filter) and the 5-minute held-only sweep (`cron.alter_job` on job id 9, explicit class-list addition)
- `supabase/migrations/20260912140000_add_narration_person_mismatch_detector.sql`, `supabase/migrations/20260912141500_wire_narration_person_mismatch_selfheal.sql` — local migration files for the above (the DB-side changes were applied live via `apply_migration` first; these are the matching repo-tracked copies)
